26-110 (G) Resolution Declaring May Motorcycle Awareness Month. Moved by Councilperson

Campbell and seconded by Councilperson J Jones.

4

May 19, 2026, Regular Meeting Draft M Minutes

WHEREAS, the City of P Pontiac is committed to promoting roadway safety for all residents, visitors, and

commuters; and

WHEREAS, motorcycles are a common and important m mode of t transportation, recreation, and economic

activity throughout t the State of M Michigan and the City of P Pontiac; and

WHEREAS, motorcyclists are more vulnerable on roadways due t to their smaller size and reduced

visibility compared to other v vehicles; and

WHEREAS, increasing public awareness of m motorcycles on roadways can r reduce accidents, injuries, and

fatalities by encouraging all drivers to share the road responsibly; and

WHEREAS, Motorcycle Awareness Month provides an opportunity t to remind motorists to remain

attentive, check blind spots, and give motorcycles the space and re~m:lP1Jhey deserve; and

WHEREAS, the Pontiac City Council supports efforts to impro".,e,~¥6Ji'c safety, protect m motorcyclists,

and promote r responsible driving behaviors within t the commuµity;> (

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT R RESOLVED, that t the PontiacCity.CQ~~H hereby declares the month of

May as Motorcycle Awareness Month in t the City of P Po]J.ti~biafid ',,,ltt;\)>t

BE I IT F FURTHER R RESOLVED, that all residents are,.ehpp,rtraged to incre~~~~their awareness of

motorcycles on the road and to practice safe drivi11ghagits to help prevent'1voi'dable accidents; and

BE I IT F FURTHER R RESOLVED, that t the City of P P~4ti4c j joins c communities a~ross the nation in

promoting motorcycle safety and awareness initiative~ during t the 19-qµth ofMay''and;throughout t the year.

Exhibit A

Ordinance No. 2495

An Ordinance t to a appropriate t the s sums of money n necessary t to meet t the e expenditures

set f forth in the budget recommended f for t the o operation of t the City o of Pontiac,

Michigan; t to defray t the d debts, expenditures, and liabilities o of s said City f for t the

fiscal y year beginning t the f first day o of J July 2 2026.

Whereas, the proposed General A Appropriations Act is required to be effective July 1, 2026,

so the City c can legally o operate.

The City o of Pontiac Ordains:

Section 1. T Title.

This ordinance shall be known as the City o of Pontiac 2026-2027 General A Appropriations A Act.

• S• Section 2. Public Hearing o on the Budget.

Pursuant to MCL 141.412 and 141.413, notice of a public hearing on the proposed budget

was published in the Oakland Press, a newspaper o of g general circulation April 27, 2026, and

a public hearing on the proposed budget w was held on May 5, 2026 and May 19, 2026.

Section 3 3. Millage Levy, Administration Fee, and Penalties.

The City C Council f for t the C City o of P Pontiac shall a authorize t the f following millages t to be levied and

collected on the general property t tax of all real and personal property w within the City upon

the current t tax roll: an allocated millage of 10.8869 operating; 1.3606 capital improvement;

2. 7 7215 sanitation; 0.4826 senior services; 1.4483 youth center; and an additional voted

operating millage of 1.0841. T The City T Treasurer is hereby a authorized to impose a o one percent

(1 %) property tax administration fee for all property taxes due, a three percent (3%) late

penalty charge when applicable and a one percent (1 %) monthly interest charge when

applicable in conformance w with Section 44 of Public A Act 2 206 of 1893.

Section 4. Adoption of Budget by Function.

The City Council of t the City of Pontiac received a recommended budget on April 16, 2026,

from Mayor Mike McGuinness. The administration has incorporated changes requested by

City C Council d during v various public presentations by t the departments. T The City C Council o of t the

City of Pontiac adopts the 2026-2027 fiscal year budgets for the various funds by

department. City officials responsible for the expenditures authorized in the budget may

expend City funds up to, but not to exceed, the total appropriation authorized for each

department.

Section 5. Payment of Bills.

Pursuant t to t the Pontiac Home Rule Charter, t the Michigan Uniform Budgeting a and A Accounting

Act (Public Act 2 of 1968, MCL 141.421 et seq.), and the Accounting Procedures Manual f for

Local Governments in Michigan, all claims (bills) against the City shall be approved by the

Finance Director o of t the City o of Pontiac prior t to being paid.

Section 6. Budgeted Revenues and Expenditures

Estimated t total r revenues and expenditures, including t transfers in and out a and other s sources,

for the various funds of the City of Pontiac beginning July 1, 2026, are $126.2 million in

revenues and $146.4 million in expenditures.

Section 7. Specific A Appropriations.

There are no specific appropriations contained in the budget.

Section 8. Periodic Financial Reports.

The Finance Director s shall p provide t the Mayor a and City C Council f financial reports on a m monthly

basis.

Section 9. Budget Monitoring a and Amending.

Whenever it appears to the Finance Director t that the actual and probable revenues in any

fund will be less than the estimated revenues upon which appropriations from such fund

were based, and w when it a appears t that e expenditures w will e exceed an appropriation upon w which

appropriations from such fund were based, the Finance Director s shall present t to the Mayor

recommendations to prevent expenditures from exceeding available revenues or

appropriations for the fiscal year. Such recommendations shall include proposals for

reducing appropriations, increasing revenues or both. The Finance Director is hereby

authorized t to make budget a amendments w within a d department i in amounts t that d do not e exceed

ten t thousand dollars ($10,000) w within a  a f fiscal y year. Any budget a amendments t to a  a d department

that e exceed ten t thousand dollars ($1 0,000)within a f fiscal y year must b be approved by t the City

Council prior to amendment. All transfers in and transfers out between appropriation

departments exceeding ten thousand dollars ($10,000) must be approved by City Council

prior t to such transfer.

Section 1 O O. Severability.

If a any s section, clause, or p provision of t this O Ordinance s shall b be declared t to be unconstitutional,

void, or illegal by any Court of competent jurisdiction, such section, clause, or provision

declared to be unconstitutional, void, or illegal shall thereby cease to be a part of this

Ordinance, but t the remainder o of t this Ordinance shall s stand and be in full f force and effect.

Section 11. Repealer.

All Ordinances or parts of Ordinances in conflict herewith are hereby repealed only t to the

extent necessary t to g give this Ordinance full f force and effect.

Section 12. Publication.

The Clerk s shall publish t this Ordinance in a newspaper o of g general circulation.

Section 13. Effective Date.

This Ordinance w was passed on May 19, 2026.

profound l loss for her f family and f for our c community; now,

PONTIAC CITY COUNCIL RESOLUTION

Recognizing J Juneteenth

in t the City of P Pontiac

Whereas, Juneteenth is celebrated in recognition of J June 19, 1865 as the date on w which

news of the end of slavery reached the slaves in the southwestern states of the United

States of America; and,

Whereas, news of t the e end of slavery did not r reach t the f frontier area of t the United States,

in particular the State of Texas and other southwestern states, until months after the

conclusion of the American Civil War, more than two and a half years after President

Abraham L Lincoln i issued t the E Emancipation P Proclamation o on J January 1, 1863; and,

Whereas, on June 19, 1865, Union soldiers, led by Major General Gordon Granger,

arrived in Galveston, Texas with n news that t the Civil War had ended and the enslaved

were f free; and,

, Whereas, African Americans who had b been slaves in t the southwest c celebrated J June 19,

commonly k known a as J Juneteenth I Independence D Day, as inspiration and encouragement

for future generations, and African Americans from the southwest continued the

tradition observing Juneteenth t through t the generations that f followed; and,

Whereas, Juneteenth has continued to grow in w widespread familiarity and celebration

throughout t the e entire c country a and i is now a recognized federal holiday; now,

Therefore, Be It Resolved, the City Council, in partnership with Mayor McGuinness,

hereby r recognizes J June 19, 2026 as J Juneteenth i in t the C City of P Pontiac; and f further

Resolved, the C City C Council w warmly w welcomes t the m many p programs a and o observances t that

are b being p planned i in t the City of Pontiac to mark J Juneteenth t this year; and f further

Resolved, the City of Pontiac supports the continued celebration of Juneteenth to

provide an opportunity for the people of Pontiac and the United States to learn more

about t the p past a and t to better u understand t this important, dark e era i in h history.

ARTICLES. O OF I INCORPORATION

OF

CATHOLIC COMMUNITY RESPONSE TEAM

(a Michigan Nonprofit Corporation)

Pursuant t to the provisions of A Act 162, Public Acts of 1 1982, as amended (the "Act"), the

undersigned Corporation executes the following Articles:

ARTICLE I

The name of t the Corporation i is CATHOLIC COMMUNITY RESPONSE TEAM.

ARTICLE II

The Corporation is organized exclusively for charitable, educational and scientific

purposes as described in Section 501(c)(3) of t the Internal Revenue Code (the "Code"), providing

direct support and referral services to those in need in t the areas of m mental health, homelessness,

housing, basic needs, identification documentation, transportation and similar services and

conducting all activities incidental or necessary to accomplishing the foregoing purposes or

otherwise p permitted b by S Section 501(c)(3) of t the Code.

ARTICLE III

The Corporation will at all times be conducted as an organization descdbed in Section

50l(c)(3) of t the Code. The Corporation will not canyon any activities which are not p permitted

to be carried on by (a) a corporation exempt from federal income tax under Section 501(c)(3) of

the Code, (b) a corporation eligible to receive tax deductible contributions under Section 170( c c)

and Section 2055, Section 2522 or Section 2106 of t the Code, or (c) a nonprofit corporation

organized u under the laws of t the State of M Michigan p pursuant t to the Act.

No part ofth~ assets or net earnings of t the Corporation may inure to the benefit of o or be

distributable to its Directors, officers, or other private persons; provided, however, that the

Corporation is authorized and empowered to pay r reasonable compensation for services rendered

and to make p payments and distributions in f furtherance of i its charitable p purposes.

No substantial part of the activities of the Corporation will be the carrying on of

propaganda or otherwise attempting to influence legislation. The Corporation will not p participate

or intervene in any political campaign on behalf o of o or in opposition to any candidate for public

office and will not p publish o or distribute statements relating to political campaigns.

ARTICLE IV

The Corporation is organized on a  a n nonstock b basis.

The Corporation h has no real p property o or p personal p property assets.

The Corporation is to be f financed through contributions and i investment i income.

The Corporation is organized on a directorship basis.

ARTICLEV

ARTICLE VII

Except as otherwise p provided b by law, a D Director or v volunteer officer of t the Corporation is

not liable to the Corporation or its members for money damages for any action taken or any

failure to take any action as a D Director o or v volunteer o officer.

The Corporation assumes all liability to any person other than the Corporation or its

of h his or h her duties as a volunteer D Director.

ARTICLE VIII

CITY COUNCIL R RESOLUTION

A RESOLUTION TO HOLD PUBLIC HEARING A AND A AUTHORIZE

THE 30-DAY P PUBLIC COMMENT P PERIOD FOR T THE 2026 A ANNUAL A ACTION PLAN

FOR T THE COMMUNITY D DEVELOPMENT B BLOCK GRANT (CDBG) A AND HOME

INVESTMENT PARTNERSHIPS (HOME) PROGRAMS

WHEREAS, the City of Pontiac is an entitlement community eligible to receive federal

Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) and HOME Investment Partnerships Program

(HOME) funds from the U.S. Department of H Housing and Urban Development (HUD); and,

WHEREAS, HUD regulations require the City to prepare and submit an Annual Action Plan

outlining the proposed use of f federal entitlement funds for Program Year 2026; and,

WHEREAS, the City has prepared a draft 2026 Annual Action Plan, which includes a proposed

budget and activities funded through the estimated allocation of $ $1,399,738.00 in CDBG funds

and $428,550.91 in HOME funds; and,

WHEREAS, the draft 2026 Annual Action Plan will be made available for public review and

comment for a 30-day period beginning June 16, 2026, through July 16, 2026, in accordance with

HUD regulations and the City's Citizen Participation Plan; and,

WHEREAS, a duly noticed public hearing was held on June 16, 2026, during a regular City

Council meeting to solicit public comments regarding the proposed activities and funding

allocations contained within the 2026 A Annual A Action Plan; and,

WHEREAS, the City has complied with the applicable requirements of the Housing and

Community Development A Act of 1974, as amended, and all related federal regulations; now,

THEREFORE, BE I IT RESOLVED, that t the City Council of t the City of P Pontiac hereby authorizes

the release of t the draft 2026 Annual Action Plan for the required 30-day public comment p period;

and further

RESOLVED, the City Council welcomes and encourages public participation during the public

review and comment p period; and further

RESOLVED, that the City shall continue to comply with all applicable federal laws, regulations,

and program requirements in the administration and implementation of t the activities identified in

the 2026 A Annual A Action Plan.

Overview

The City Hall Parking Lot Lighting project will totally rehabilitate the antiquated and obsolete parking lot

lighting i in t the m main p parking l lot a and street l lighting along H Hill Street. Results of t the p project w will improve safety,

visibility, and accessibility in the main parking area and along Hill Street as well as result in a reduction in

annual energy expenses due to LED lighting fixture being installed. The project will also provide a much

more reliable lighting system, and preventative as well as routine maintenance activities will become the

responsibility of D DTE Energy.

A. Customer acknowledges that all or a portion of the Equipment is Special Order

Materials (“SOM”) and not Company’s standard stock. Customer will purchase and stock

replacement SOM and spare parts as provided in Section B below. When replacement equipment

or spare parts are installed from Customer’s inventory, Company will credit Customer in the

amount of the then-current material cost of Company standard street lighting equipment in lieu of

which the SOM is being used. Customer has up to twenty-four (24) months from the time

Company uses, or takes possession of, such Customer SOM to seek reimbursement from

Company.

B. Customer will maintain an inventory of at least ___ posts and ___ luminaires and

any other materials agreed to by Company and Customer, and will replenish the stock by ordering

materials no later than thirty (30) calendar days after the materials are drawn from inventory.

Costs of initial inventory are included in this Agreement. If Customer fails to maintain the required

inventory, Company, after 30 days’ notice to Customer, may (but is not required to) order

replacement SOM and Customer will reimburse Company for its costs (including the labor costs

associated with Company’s management of the supply chain for the SOM) no later than t thirty (30)

calendar days after receipt of Company’s invoice for such costs. Customer‘s acknowledges that

failure to maintain required inventory could result in extended outages due to SOM lead times.

C. The inventory will be stored at _______________________________________.

Access to Customer’s inventory site must be provided between the hours of 9:00a.m. to 4:00p.m.,

Monday through Friday with the exceptions of federal Holidays. If Company is unable to access

the site during such hours for any reason, Company: (i) shall be relieved from any obligation or

commitment to complete the work as scheduled; and (ii) may, at its option, procure the inventory

itself and have Customer to reimburse Company’s costs for doing so. Customer shall name an

authorized representative to contact regarding inventory: levels, access, usage, transactions, and

provide the following contact information to Company:

Name: __________________________ Title: ______________________________

Phone Number:___________________ Email: _____________________________

Customer will immediately notify Company of any changes in the Authorized Customer

Representative. Customer must comply with SOM manufacturer’s recommended inventory

storage guidelines and practices. Damaged SOM will not be installed by Company.

D. In the event that SOM is damaged by a third party, Company may (but is not

required to) pursue a damage claim against such third party for all of Company’s costs incurred

because of the claim, including all labor and replacement materials. Company will notify

Customer as to whether Company will pursue such claim within a reasonable time of the SOM

being damaged.

E. In the event that SOM becomes obsolete, discontinued, or incompatible with

Company’s infrastructure, Customer shall select new alternate SOM that is compatible with

Company’s then-existing infrastructure.  If Customer does not select compatible alternate SOM,

Company reserves the right to select compatible SOM that is, in its reasonable judgment,

substantially similar, or replace the SOM with standard materials, in either case being entitled to

Purchase Agreement – Page 3

reimbursement from Customer for Company’s costs in providing such transition of supply

(including internal overhead and labor costs).

F. Should Customer experience, in Company’s reasonable judgment, excessive LED

equipment failures that are not supported by LED manufacturer warranties, Company will replace

the LED equipment with other Company supported Solid State or High Intensity Discharge

luminaires at Company’s discretion. The full cost to complete these replacements to standard

street lighting equipment will be the responsibility of Customer.

Pontiac City Hall Lighting Upgrade

May 13, 2026

DTE

City Hall Lighting Upgrade – Overview

This City Hall lighting upgrade strengthens the partnership between DTE and

the City of Pontiac while improving safety, visibility, and accessibility in

the main parking area and along Hill StreetThe project delivers immediate

.

benefits to residents, staff, and visitors and establishes a foundation for

addressing aging city-owned lighting infrastructure. It also positions

DTE and the City to identify priorities and advance long-term, citywide

streetlighting improvements that support safety, reliability, and

community growth.

2

• Removals

• Removal of 19 existing light poles,

including:

• 12 fixtures within the concrete parking area

• 7 fixtures located in adjacent green space

• Supports modernization and transition to new,

upgraded lighting system

• Includes full hard surface restoration of all

disturbed concrete areas

• Ensures parking lot is returned to a clean,

uniform, and finished condition following

installation

• *Please note: Removal of the lights may affect other lighting or electrical services within

the shared conduit system. While some services may require re-feeding as a result, this

work is not included in DTE’s scope for this project.

• Photometric-based lighting design

consistent, uniform illumination across the City

Hall campus parking area

• Optimized layout enhances

and overall light distribution

• Extends lighting coverage to

of Hill Street

• Improves safety for both drivers and

pedestrians in and around the corridor

• Addresses long-standing gap by

the pedestrian connection between City

Hall and buildings east of Hill Street

CKS Service will implement an updated automation system and reconfigure existing audio

equipment a and access p points w within t the Council Chambers and Cable Control Room t to enable r real

CITY OF PONTIAC CITY COUNCIL

RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING CONTRACT WITH CKS SERVICE FOR

AUDIONISUAL RECONFIGURATION SERVICES FOR COUNCIL CHAMBERS

AND CABLE CONTROL R ROOM

WHEREAS, the City of P Pontiac operates Cable access facilities, including the Council Chambers and Cable

Room, which require functional and up-to-date production technology infrastructure to support public meetings

and live recordings; and,

WHEREAS, the existing audio/visual systems within the Council Chambers and Cable Control Room require

reconfiguration to improve operational efficiency and enable real-time adjustments during meetings and live

broadcasts; and,

WHEREAS, CKS Service has been identified as a sole source p provider f for these services as the original installer

of t the City's audio/visual system, possessing p proprietary knowledge of t the existing equipment and configuration,

engaging an alternative vendor would risk system incompatibility, potential loss of w warranty coverage, project

complications, and significant loss of t time; and,

WHEREAS, CKS Service has submitted a, qualified quote to implement an updated automation system and

reconfigure existing audio equipment and access points within the Council Chambers and Cable Control Room

and this sole source c contract p provides t the City of P Pontiac with t the ability t to utilize t the vendor t that h has t the existing,

specialized familiarity with t the challenging c current configurations of t the audio/visual engineering w within Council

Chambers and the Cable Control Room; and,

WHEREAS, this project will establish a cohesive technology ecosystem between the Cable Control Room and

Council Chambers, and supports the City's long-term plan for continued upgrades to production technology

infrastructure in both facilities; and,

WHEREAS, t the Pontiac City Council h has previously approved a b budget amendment appropriating $22,785 from

Cable Funds for the purpose of f funding said services; and,

WHEREAS, pursuant to City purchasing policy, contracts valued at $10,000 or more require prior approval of

the City Council before execution; and,

WHEREAS, the funding sources for this project are GL Account 231-291-977.014: Cable Funds and is funded

by previously approved budget amendments approved by City Council; now,

THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, t that the Pontiac City Council hereby authorizes the Mayor o or h his designee

to enter into a c contract w with CKS Service for professional audio/visual engineering reconfiguration services in an

amount not t to exceed $22,785.

CITY OF PONTIAC

OFFICIAL MEMORANDUM

TO: Honorable City Council President and City Council Members

FROM: Larry Sirls, Director, Department of P Public Works

CC: Mike McGuinness, Mayor and Richard T. Seay, Deputy Mayor

DATE: June 16, 2026

RE: Resolution Authorizing Reimbursement R Request Under Oakland County's 2026 West

Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement P Program

CITY COUNCIL RESOLUTION SUMMARY:

Resolution Authorizing Reimbursement Request Under Oakland

County's 2026 West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement

RESOLUTION TITLE Program

DEPARTMENT Department o of P Public Works

CONTRACT

NUMBER(S) NI A I A - Reimbursement P Program

TYPE Reimbursement Agreement

CONTRACT TERM NIA

RENEW A AL OPTIONS No Renewal Options

Oakland County West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement

FUNDING SOURCE Program

GL FUNDING SOURCE NIA

TOTAL NOT-TO

EXCEED AMOUNT $7,886.16

Overview

Oakland County has established a West Nile prevention reimbursement program. The City of

Pontiac is eligible for $7,886.16 in funds this year. The City has developed a 2026 mosquito control

program that includes the application of larvicide in catch basins throughout Pontiac and the

distribution of p personal insect r repellent t to residents. The total cost for the City's p proposed program

for 2026 is $7,886.16. City Council approval of t this resolution is required by Oakland County to

qualify for reimbursement u under t the 2026 West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement Program.

Recommendation

Department of Public Works staff recommend that the City Council approve this resolution

authorizing t the Director o of t the Department of P Public Works to act as agent for the City of P Pontiac in

requesting reimbursement of eligible mosquito control activities in an amount not to exceed

$7,886.16 under Oakland County's 2026 West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement P Program.

CITY OF P PONTIAC CITY COUNCIL

RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING REIMBURSEMENT REQUEST UNDER

OAKLAND COUNTY'S 2026 WEST NILE VIRUS PREVENTION

REIMBURSEMENT PROGRAM

WHEREAS, upon the recommendation of t the Oakland County Executive, the Oakland County

Board of C Commissioners has established a West Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement Program

to assist Oakland County cities, villages, and townships in addressing mosquito control activities;

and,

WHEREAS, Oakland County's West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement Program authorizes

Oakland County cities, villages, and townships to apply for reimbursement of e eligible expenses

incurred in connection with personal mosquito protection measures, habitat eradication,

larviciding, or allowable related activities in designated community areas; and,

WHEREAS, the City of P Pontiac will be incurring expenses in the summer of 2 2026 in connection

with mosquito control activities, including the application of l larvicide in catch basins throughout

the City and the distribution of p personal insect repellent to residents, believed to be eligible for

reimbursement under Oakland County's 2026 West Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement

Program; and,

WHEREAS, the total expenditures for eligible mosquito control activities shall not exceed

$7,886.16, consistent w with t the reimbursement amount a allocated t to the City of P Pontiac for the 2026

program year; and,

WHEREAS, City Council approval of this resolution is required by Oakland County as a

condition of r reimbursement u under t the 2026 West N Nile Virus Prevention R Reimbursement P Program;

now,

THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that t the Pontiac City Council hereby authorizes and directs

the Director of t the Department of P Public Works to act on behalf o of t the City of P Pontiac, in the

manner and to the extent provided under the Oakland County Board of Commissioners'

Miscellaneous Resolution, to request reimbursement of e eligible mosquito control activities under

Oakland County's 2026 West N Nile Virus Prevention Reimbursement Program; and further

RESOLVED, that the total expenditures for eligible mosquito control activities shall not exceed

Seven Thousand, Eight Hundred Eighty-Six Dollars and Sixteen Cents ($7,886.16), consistent

with the reimbursement amount allocated to the City of P Pontiac for the 2026 program year.

Eligible & In& Ineligible Expenditures

Eligible Expenditures

• Larvicide for catch basins, retention ponds, etc. (product only)

• Larvicide for distribution to residents (pre-packaged larvicide must be distributed as packaged)

• Personal insect repellent for distribution to residents (eligible products must contain DEET, Picaridin,

IR3535, Oil of Lemon Eucalyptus, para-menthane-diol, or permethrin as the active ingredient). Product

purchased must be individual-sized (wipes or lotion/sprays in 6 o oz or smaller containers).

• Consider providing insect repellent products during local festivals, to parks & r recreation

programs, and senior centers

• Distribute repellent products to multiple locations

Ineligible Expenditures

• Cost of staff t time, overtime or equipment used to distribute larvicide

• Adulticide

• Cost of protective clothing, boots, etc.

• Clean-up days, dumpster rentals, etc.

• Citronella candles, yard foggers, bug zappers, coils, lamps or similar items

• Health educational materials

• Local newsletter

If y you have questions regarding product eligibility, contact Signa Metivier at metiviers@oakgov.com before ordering.

RE: Resolution Authorizing the City Clerk to Publish Notice of a  a Q4 Fiscal Year

End Budget A Amendment (FY 2025-2026 True-Up)

The F Finance D Department i is submitting t the attached r resolution authorizing t the City Clerk t to publish

notice of a  a Q4 fiscal year-end budget amendment for FY 2025-2026. This is a year-end true-up.

First, it p provides General Fund t transfers to bring whole the funds projected t to close t the fiscal year

in a deficit p position; and second, it increases appropriations in t the departments and funds projected

to end the year with expenditures over their adopted appropriations. Each adjustment is offset

within the affected fund by available fund balance, revenues not previously appropriated, or an

operating transfer in. In total, the amendment adds an estimated $6,055,000.00 in appropriation

authority across the affected funds - $2,035,000.00 in General Fund t transfers t to deficit f funds and

$4,020,000.00 in direct appropriation increases. The amendment is summarized below:

• All current funds projected to end FY 2025-2026 in a deficit position - estimated

total: $2,035,000.00

• All current expenditures projected to end FY 2025-2026 exceeding appropriations -

estimated total: $4,020,000.00

1

Summary-Use o of F Fund Balance by Fund:

Fund Balance Used

Fund 101 - General Fund $2,310,000.00

Fund 232 - VEBA Retiree Healthcare Opt Out $3,720,000.00

Fund 212 - Senior Activities Millage $25,000.00

Total Fund Balance Used $6,055,000.00

Fund JOI - General Fund use comprises a $135,000.00 operating transfer to the Cable Fund, a $1,900,000.00

operating transfer to the District Court, and  $275,000.00 f0 for Waterford  Fire Department  professional  services.

This amendment is required to comply with the City Charter and the Uniform Budgeting and

Accounting Act. Charter Section 5.104 requires that total appropriations not exceed the City's

estimated revenues and that no liabilities be incurred except in accordance with the appropriation

ordinance. The Uniform Budgeting and Accounting Act, Act 2 of 1968 (MCL 141.421 et seq.),

prohibits deficit spending: it bars adopting an appropriations act or amendment that causes total

estimated expenditures, including an accrued deficit, to exceed total estimated revenues and

available surplus (MCL 141.436(7)); prohibits incurring expenditures against an appropriation

account beyond the amount appropriated (MCL 141.438(3)); and requires the legislative body to

amend the appropriations act as soon as a deviation becomes apparent and its amount can be

determined (MCL 141.437(1)).

ORDINANCE

CITY OF PONTIAC

ORDINANCE

NON-STORMWATER D DISCHARGES

AN ORDINANCE TO AMEND CHAPTER 58, ENVIRONMENT, OF THE CODE OF

ORDINANCES FOR THE CITY OF PONTIAC, NON-STORMWATER DISCHARGES,

IN ORDER TO REGULATE THE DISCHARGE OF NON-STORMWATER

SUBSTANCES AND PROTECT WATER QUALITY STANDARDS IN THE CITY OF

PONTIAC.

THE CITY OF PONTIAC ORDAINS:

Section 1. Amendment.

Chapter 58, Environment, of t the Code of O Ordinances for the City of P Pontiac, shall be

amended by adding a new Article IX to read as follows:

ARTICLE IX. - - NON-STORMWATER D DISCHARGES

Sec. 58-700. - Definitions.

For t the purposes of t this article, the words set o out shall have the following meanings:

Discharge means the addition of a any waste, waste effluent, wastewater, pollutant, or any

combination thereof t to any surface water of t the state.

Municipal Separate Storm Sewer S System (MS4) means all separate storm sewers t that are owned or

operated by the United States, a state, city, township, county, district, association, or other p public

body created by or pursuant to state law, having j jurisdiction over disposal of s sewage, industrial

wastes, storm water, or other wastes, including special districts under state law, such as a sewer

district, flood control district, or drainage district, or similar entity, or a designated or approved

management agency under Section 208 of t the Clean Water A Act t that discharges to the waters of t the

state. This term includes systems similar t to separate storm sewer systems in municipalities, such

as systems at military bases, large hospital or p prison complexes, and highways and thoroughfares.

The t term d does n not i include separate storm sewers in v very d discrete areas, such as individual buildings.

NP D DES m means National Pollutant D Discharge Elimination System.

Storm water means storm water runoff, snow melt runoff, surface runoff and drainage, and non

storm water included under t the conditions of t this article.

Waters of thf the State means any of t the following: groundwater; lakes, including t the Great Lakes

bordering the state; rivers; streams; all other water courses and bodies of w water within the

jurisdiction of the state, including wetlands as defined by Part 303 of the Act, wetlands

protection, being SS324.30301 to 324.30323 of t the Michigan Compiled Laws.

Sec. 58-701. - Unauthorized discharges.

a) No person shall discharge or cause to be discharged into the MS4 or waters of t the state

of any materials, including but not limited to pollutants or waters containing any

pollutants that cause or contribute to a violation of a applicable water quality standards,

other than storm water. The commencement, conduct, or continuance of any illicit

discharge t to t the MS4 is prohibited; however, the following discharges are excluded from

prohibition as described so long as they are identified as not being a significant

contributor of p pollutants to the waters of t the state:

(1) Water line flushing and discharges from potable water sources;

(2) Landscape irrigation runoff, lawn watering runoff, and irrigation waters;

(3) Diverted stream flows and flows from riparian habitats or wetlands;

( 4 4) Rising groundwaters and springs;

(5) Uncontaminated groundwater infiltration and seepage;

(6) Uncontaminated pumped groundwater, except for groundwater

cleanups specifically authorized by NPDES permits;

(7) Foundation drains, water f from crawl space pumps, footing drains,

and basement sumps;

(8) Air conditioning condensation;

(9) Waters from noncommercial car washing;

(10) Street w wash water; and

(11) Dechlorinated swimming pool water from single, two and three

family residences.

b) Discharges or f flows from firefighting activities are not p prohibited unless they are

identified as a significant source of p pollutants t to the waters of t the state.

Sec. 58-702. - Prohibition of i illicit c connections.

a) The construction, use, maintenance, or continued existence of i illicit connections t to the

storm drain system is p prohibited.

b) This p prohibition e expressly i includes, w without l limitation, illicit c connections m made in t the p past,

regardless of w whether the connection was permissible under l law o or p practices applicable

or p prevailing at t the t time o of c connection.

c) A person is considered to be in violation of t this article if t the person connects a line

conveying sewage to a storm drain system or MS4 or allows such a connection to

continue.

Sec. 58-703. - Prohibition of D Direct D Dumping or D Disposal of M Materials into the M MS4.

The direct dumping of m materials or discharges into the MS4 is prohibited except for those

illicit discharges identified as not b being a significant contributor t to violations of w water q quality

standards.

Sec. 58-704. - Right o of E Entry:

The City of P Pontiac D Department of P Public W Works or other authorized enforcement agency shall

be permitted to enter upon all properties for the p purposes of i inspection, observation, measurement,

sampling and testing of suspected non-stormwater discharges in accordance with the

provisions of t this article. Refusal ofreasonable access to t the City and/or r representatives of

the authorized e enforcement a agency t to any p part o of t the p premises i is a v violation of t this article.

Section 2. Repealer.

This Ordinance hereby repeals any ordinances in conflict h herewith.

Section 3. Savings Clause.

That nothing in this Ordinance hereby adopted be construed to affect any j just or legal right or

remedy of a any character nor shall any j just or legal right or remedy of a any character be lost,

impaired or affected b by this Ordinance.

Section 4. Severability.

The various parts, Sections and clauses of t this Ordinance are declared to be severable. If a any

part, sentence, paragraph, Section or clause is adjudged unconstitutional or i invalid by a court of

competent j jurisdiction, the remainder of t the Ordinance shall not be affected.

Section 5. Adoption and Effective Date.

This Ordinance is hereby declared to have been adopted

Pontiac at a m meeting t thereof d duly called and held on the

be given effect as mandated by City and statute.

by the City Council for the City of

16th day of J June, - -2026, and ordered t to

ORDINANCE

CITY OF PONTIAC CITY COUNCIL

EMERGENCY MUNICIPAL ORDINANCE

AN EMERGENCY INTERIM ZONING ORDINANCE TO AMEND CHAPTER 42,

COMMUNITY DEVELOPMENT, OF THE PONTIAC MUNICIPAL CODE TO ADD

ARTICLE XI, TEMPORARY MORATORIUM ON NEW OR EXPANDED DETENTION

FACILITIES, AND TO ESTABLISH A TEMPORARY LAND USE MORATORIUM ON

THE ACCEPTANCE, PROCESSING, AND APPROVAL OF APPLICATIONS FOR NEW

OR EXPANDED DETENTION FACILITIES IN THE CITY OF PONTIAC TO THE

MAXIMUM EXTENT PERMITTED BYLAW

CHAPTER 42, ARTICLE XI. MORATORIUM ON THE ACCEPTANCE, PROCESSING, AND

APPROVAL OF APPLICATIONS FOR N NEW OR E EXPANDED DETENTION F FACILITIES

THE CITY OF PONTIAC ORDAINS:

Add Chapter 42, Article XI, Section 42-258. Purpose and Findings

A. The City of P Pontiac has determined that an emergency interim zoning ordinance is necessary to

ensure that the City has adequate time to study and consider appropriate zoning and land use

regulations for new or expanded detention facilities within t the City.

B. The City has a substantial interest in protecting the public peace, property, health, safety, and

welfare of i its residents, and in ensuring the orderly development and use of land within the

City. The City has further determined that new or expanded detention facilities may create

significant land use impacts, including, but not limited to, impacts on public safety, emergency

services, transportation, parking, construction staging, utilities, water and sewer capacity,

wastewater systems, public health, and compatibility with surrounding land uses.

C. The City Code and Zoning Ordinance do not currently contain regulations specifically

addressing detention facilities as a separate land use. The City has determined that additional

study is necessary to evaluate whether and how detention facilities should be regulated through

the City's zoning and development review processes, including the appropriate zoning districts,

approval standards, site design requirements, public safety access, buffering, parking, traffic

circulation, utility impacts, and other land use considerations.

D. The City has considered the consequences of h having no moratorium, but has determined that a

temporary moratorium is necessary to preserve the status quo while the City studies and

considers appropriate zoning and land use regulations for detention facilities. The City further

finds that an emergency ordinance is necessary because delay could result in the filing,

acceptance, processing, or approval of applications for new or expanded detention facilities

before the City has adequate regulations to evaluate t the land use impacts of s such facilities.

E. This Ordinance is adopted as an emergency interim zoning and land use regulation and shall

temporarily supplement the City of P Pontiac Zoning Ordinance and all City application, permit,

site plan, special exception, certificate of u use, occupancy, building, and development approval

procedures for the duration of t the moratorium.

F. This Ordinance is intended to operate as an interim zoning and land use regulation. It is not

intended to regulate detention operations, custody decisions, immigration enforcement,

criminal j justice operations, conditions of c confinement, or t the internal operations of a any federal,

state, county, or other governmental entity.

Add Chapter 42, Article X XI, Section 42-259. Moratorium

A. The City hereby places a temporary moratorium on the acceptance, processing, review, or

approval of any application, permit, site plan, special exception permit, zoning approval,

building permit, occupancy permit, certificate of use, temporary use permit, or other

development approval for the establishment of a  a new detention facility or the expansion of a an

existing detention facility within the City of P Pontiac.

B. During the moratorium, no City department, official, employee, board, commission, or body

shall accept, process, review, approve, or issue any application, permit, approval, or

authorization for the establishment of a  a new detention facility or the expansion of a an existing

detention facility, whether t the proposed use is principal or accessory, and whether t the proposed

use is permanent or t temporary.

C. For purposes of this Ordinance, "expansion" means an increase in the size, area, capacity,

number of b beds, intensity, quantity, or scope of a an existing detention facility use.

D. Any application or request submitted during the moratorium that seeks approval for a use or

activity prohibited by this Ordinance shall not be accepted or processed. Any application

accepted as a result of error, omission, vague description, or incomplete or inaccurate

information shall be null and void and shall have no legal force or e effect.

Add Chapter 42, Article X XI, Section 42-260. Moratorium Term.

A. The moratorium established by this Ordinance shall remain in effect for a period of o one (1)

year from the effective date of this Ordinance, unless repealed, shortened, or extended by

subsequent action of t the City Council as permitted by law.

B. During the moratorium, the City Administration, Planning Division, and any other appropriate

City officials or consultants shall study and make recommendations concerning permanent

zoning and land use regulations for detention facilities. The study may include, but is not

limited t to, analysis of t the following:

1. The potential land use impacts of d detention facilities;

2. Appropriate zoning districts for detention facilities, if a any;

3. Whether detention facilities should be permitted as a principal permitted use, special

exception use, conditional use, or other regulated use;

4. Site design, access, circulation, parking, loading, security, buffering, screening, lighting,

utility, water, sewer, wastewater, emergency access, and public safety considerations;

5. Construction staging and temporary impacts associated with the development or expansion

of d detention facilities;

6. The relationship between detention facilities and surrounding residential, commercial,

industrial, institutional, civic, and public uses;

7. Whether different t types of d detention facilities should be regulated differently based on their

land use impacts; and

8. Any other zoning, planning, public safety, infrastructure, or land use considerations

determined to be relevant.

C. No later than nine (9) months after the effective date of t this Ordinance, the Mayor or designee

shall transmit the results of t the study, together with any recommended ordinance amendments,

to the City Council for consideration.

Add Chapter 42, Article XI, Section 42-261. Definition

A. For purposes of t this Ordinance only, "detention facility" means a facility used for the secure

confinement of persons who are detained pending adjudication, detained pending civil

immigration proceedings, or serving a sentence of i incarceration.

B. For purposes of t this Ordinance only, "detention facility" does not include:

C. A facility operated solely for the detention of jf juveniles f for rehabilitative, counseling, treatment,

mental health, educational, or medical services under the j jurisdiction of t the Michigan Family

Court or t the Michigan Department of H Health and Human Services;

D. A temporary holding cell within a police station or courthouse used exclusively for pre

arraignment, in-custody court appearances, or other temporary law enforcement or court

related custody lasting less than seventy-two (72) hours;

E. An influx care facility for unaccompanied noncitizen children operated by the United States

Department of H Health and Human Services Office of R Refugee Resettlement; or

F. A receiving center for commercially sexually exploited children under Michigan law, MCL

712B.1 et seq.

G. For purposes of t this Ordinance only, the terms ''jail" and ''jail farm," wherever used in the

Pontiac City Code or Z Zoning Ordinance, shall be treated as detention facilities.

Add Chapter 42, Article XI, Section 42-262. Existing Lawful Uses; Governmental Uses;

Preemption Savings Clause

A. This Ordinance shall not prohibit the lawful continuation, maintenance, or repair of a any vested

and otherwise lawfully established use, structure, or development existing as of t the effective

date of t this Ordinance, provided that such use, structure, or development is not expanded

during the moratorium.

B. This Ordinance is intended to regulate land use and development approvals to the maximum

extent permitted by law. Nothing in this Ordinance shall be interpreted or applied to regulate

the conditions of c confinement, detention operations, immigration enforcement, criminal j justice

operations, law enforcement operations, or any federal, state, county, or other governmental

function in a manner preempted by state or federal law.

C. Nothing in this Ordinance shall be construed to prohibit, restrict, or impair a federal, state,

county, or o other governmental entity from carrying out a governmental function where the City

lacks authority t to apply its zoning or land use regulations under applicable law.

D. Nothing in this Ordinance shall be construed to prohibit ordinary maintenance, repairs, life

safety work, security improvements, code compliance work, or other work required to protect

health, safety, or w welfare at an existing lawful facility, provided such work does not expand the

detention facility use unless otherwise required by law.

E. To the extent any application of t this Ordinance to a particular facility, person, governmental

entity, or circumstance would be preempted by state or federal law, or would otherwise be

unenforceable under applicable law, the Ordinance shall be applied only to the maximum

extent p permitted by law, and the invalid or preempted application shall not affect t the validity or

enforceability of t this Ordinance in any other r respect.

Add Chapter 42, Article X XI, Section 42-263. Validity and Severability

A. If a any section, subsection, sentence, clause, phrase, word, or application of t this Ordinance is

held invalid, unconstitutional, unenforceable, or preempted by state or federal law, such

holding shall not affect the validity of t the remaining portions or applications of t this Ordinance,

which shall remain in full force and effect.

Add Chapter 42, Article X XI, Section 42-264. Conflict w with Other Ordinances

A. All ordinances or parts of o ordinances in conflict with this Ordinance are repealed only to the

extent n necessary to give this Ordinance full force and effect during the moratorium term.

Add Chapter 42, Article X XI, Section 42-265. Emergency Clause and Effective Date

A. The City Council finds and declares that an emergency exists because any delay in the effective

date of this Ordinance could allow the acceptance, processing, review, or approval of

applications for new or expanded detention facilities before the City has adequate zoning and

land use regulations to evaluate the impacts of such facilities on the public peace, property,

health, safety, and welfare.

B. This Ordinance is declared to be an emergency ordinance necessary for the immediate

preservation of t the public peace, property, health, safety, and welfare of t the City of P Pontiac.

C. This Ordinance shall become effective immediately upon adoption as authorized by Section

3.l 1 12(e) of t the City of P Pontiac Home Rule Charter.

Overview

The City of P Pontiac has acquired the 300 W. Huron Building and the Department of P Public Works

requires technology infrastructure upgrades to bring the facility to City operational standards.

The project addresses four areas: camera systems (interior and exterior), door access control,

structured cabling, and IT hardware. All vendors are currently u under contract w with t the City and h have

been sourced through the City's p procurement p process.

Scope Vendor Amount

Camera Systems DIA C Central $225,230.00

Door A Access Systems DIA C Central $75,450.00

Structured Cabling BND (Best N Network D Design) $75,000.00

IT Hardware Insight P Public Sector Inc. $128,552.11

Total Not-to-Exceed $504,232.11

Funding

Funding f for the 300 W. Huron Technology Improvements is budgeted in GL Account N No. 101-458-

818-000. The total project c cost shall not exceed $504,232.11.

These investments in facility and campus security are prudent in order to protect the structure, the

grounds, employees, the visitors, the equipment, and more. It is also a cost-effective mesasure to

proactively address property liability, verifying or refuting any claims made agains the City of

Pontiac related to our p property, equipment, and operations.

Recommendation

The Department o of P Public Works recommends that t the City Council approve the vendor agreements

with DIA Central, BND, and Insight Public Sector Inc. for technology upgrades at 300 W. Huron

and authorize the IT Director and Director of P Public Works to execute all agreements and related

documents.

CITY COUNCIL RESOLUTION SUMMARY:

Resolution to Approve Construction Engineering Services

Agreement with Hubbell, Roth & Clark, Inc. for Citywide

Safety Improvements and Kennett Road Safety

RESOLUTION TITLE Improvements

DEPARTMENT Department o of P Public Works

TOTAL NOT-TO-EXCEED

AMOUNT $265,648.00

Overview

The City of P Pontiac was awarded $1,887,840 in Federal Highway Safety Improvement Program

(HSIP) funds, with the City contributing $387,840 as its required 20% local match. The low bid for

construction was received on May 1, 2026, for $1,688,273.29 from Dan's Excavating, Inc. The

project encompasses two improvement p packages:

• Citywide Safety Improvements: Centerpoint P Parkway at R Ring Road signal modernization;

Walton Boulevard HA W WK pedestrian hybrid beacon with median refuge island (Colony

Lane to Greenwood Lane); and sign upgrades on Edison Street at Howard, Chamberlain,

Lewis, Kemp, and Whitfield Streets.

• Kennett Road Safety Improvements: Signal modernizations at Kennett Road &

Elementary School and Kennett Road & Stanley A v venue; removal of p pedestrian bridge on

Kennett Road; wet reflective pavement markings from City Limit to Baldwin; and sign

upgrades at Sarasota Avenue, Cherrylawn Drive, Cloverlawn Drive, and Meadowlawn

Drive.

To administer the project in accordance with MDOT Local Agency Program (LAP) requirements,

a Construction E Engineering p proposal was requested from Hubbell, Roth & Clark, Inc. (HRC). HRC

will provide complete contract administration, MDOT office technician/clerk services,

AASHTOWare reporting, wage reviews, construction engineering, record drawings, construction

layout/staking, on-site observation, and materials testing.

Funding

HSIP funds cover 80% of construction; the City's 20% share ($387,840) and CE services

($265,648) are funded through the Major Street Fund, GL No. 202-451-974.000. HRC's fee

includes $240,648 in direct labor and $25,000 in subconsultant costs for mast arm fabrication and

anchor bolt t testing.

Recommendation

Department of Public Works recommends approval of the HRC Construction Engineering

agreement not to exceed $265,648 to ensure proper construction oversight, MDOT LAP

compliance, and federal closeout for both improvement p projects.

RE: Traffic Engineering Services

Pontiac, Michigan

Dear Larry:

The professional staff  of Fleis & VandenBrink Engineering, Inc. (F&V) appreciates the opportunity  to present

you with our proposal to provide Traffic Engineering Services for the City of Pontiac, Michigan. Our

understanding of  the project needs, proposed scope of  work, and associated professional fees are outlined

below.

PROJECT UNDERSTANDING

The City of Pontiac identified several key areas where they are looking for  traffic engineering support  to

accomplish specific goals regarding operations and safety. The following projects were discussed at our

meeting on January 13, 2026:

A. FIRE STATION LOCATIONS

A comprehensive review of  the existing fire stations and the existing service areas. Provide

recommendations for locating new or relocated fire stations to support operations and provide coverage.

B. EMERGENCY VEHICLE PRE-EMPTION (EVP)

Provide support  for  the City regarding the data necessary  for grant applications to implement EVP on key

corridors where the technology would be the most impactful.

C. SIGNALIZED INTERSECTION STUDY

Provide a comprehensive evaluation of  the existing signalized intersections in the City and identify potential

locations to remove traffic signals. Provide further analysis and evaluation of  the identified intersections to

determine if  the signal is warranted.

D. COMMERCIAL VEHICLE ROUTE SIGNAGE

Perform a review of  the existing truck routing in the City. Provide recommendations for a comprehensive truck

routing plan to manage local truck activity and reduce impacts on the local community. Recommendation to

include a comprehensive truck routing plan for implementation and enforcement.

Our understanding of  the project is based on the information you have provided, a review of  the study road

network, and our professional experience. If  our understanding of  this project does not  fully address your

needs, please let us know, and we will modify our proposal accordingly.

27725 Stansbury Boulevard, Suite 195

Farmington Hills, Ml 48334

P: 248.536.0080

F: 248.536.0079

P51754 City of Pontiac - Traffic Engineering Se1vices Proposal 4-28-2026 www.fveng.com

Traffic Engineering Services Proposal City of Pontiac, Michigan

April 28, 2026 I P Page 2 of  4

SCOPE OF SERVICES

A. FIRE STATION LOCATIONS

A comprehensive review of  the existing fire stations and the existing service areas. Provide

recommendations for locating new or  relocated fire stations to support operation and  provide coverage.

1. Background Information

a. Obtain the locations of  the existing fire stations from the City of Pontiac. Provide a description of  each

fire station location and its service area, including a site map showing each location.

b. Obtain historic run volumes at  the existing fire station locations. Provide a summary of  the existing

run volumes and locations.

2. Data Review and Location Assessment

a. Evaluate historic run volumes and locations and complete an evaluation to determine the preferred

facility location(s). Use Geographic Information System (GIS) data from the City of Pontiac to review

the existing available demographic and property information.

b. Identify potential building sites through the development of  ranking criteria through the established

City/Department goals. Key items in this evaluation and ranking of  site includes:

c. Determine best coverage through evaluation of  the established City criteria for population served and

miles of  street covered in the established response time.

• Population and Demographics serviced

• Call frequency

• Existing facility locations (asset management not included in this scope)

• Available vacant properties in preferred service areas

• Site preparation and associated costs

• Site Access

3. Recommendations Summary

a. Use the established criteria to prepare a list of  potential locations for new or relocated fire stations for

consideration.

b. Provide recommendations for locating new or relocated fire stations to support operation and provide

coverage.

B. EMERGENCY VEHICLE PRE-EMPTION (EVP)

Provide support for the City regarding the data necessary for  grant applications to implement EVP on key

corridors where the technology would be the most impactful.

1. Grant  Application Assistance

a. Work  with the City  to provide information supporting the grant applications for EVP implementation.

b. Several grant opportunities are available for the design and implementation of EVP technology,

including:

• Better Utilizing Investments to Leverage Development (BUILD) Grant: $5M-$25M, 80% grant

funded

• Advanced Transportation Technology and Innovation (ATTAIN) Grant: $3M-$9M, 80% grant

funded

• SS4A Planning Grant: $1 M-4M, 80% grant  funded

• CMAQ Grant: 80% grant  funded

• HSIP Grant: 80% grant  funded, Must have a safety element-reducing crashes through EVP

C. SIGNALIZED INTERSECTION STUDY

Provide a comprehensive evaluation of  the existing signalized intersections in the City and  identify  potential

locations to remove traffic signals. Provide further analysis and evaluation of  the identified intersections to

determine if  the signal is warranted.

1. Study  Area

a. Obtain information from the City regarding their existing inventory  of  signalized intersections.

b. Summarize the signalized intersections by corridor and isolated locations.

c. Obtain existing available traffic count data on the study corridors and adjacent  to the isolated

locations

~

P51754 City of  PonUac - Traffic Engineering Servlces Proposal 4-28-2026 F&V

Traffic Engineering Services Proposal City of Pontiac, Michigan

April 28, 2026 I P Page 3 of  4

d. Obtain information from the City regarding the age of  the existing signal infrastructure at  the

intersection locations.

e. Rank the intersections based upon location, traffic volumes, and the age of  the equipment  to provide

a high level determination of  which signals intersections may be considered for removal.

f. Prepare a list of  intersections to be evaluated for consideration of  removing the traffic signal. Upon

review with the City the final list  of intersections for evaluation will be determined.

g. Conduct a site visit at  the selected study intersections and collect a field inventory including the

existing geometries, lane use, pedestrian facilities and proximity  to transit. This proposal includes

an initial evaluation of up to 10 study intersections, additional intersections can be included in the

stuy  for additional fees.

h. Update the list of  recommended intersections for considering removal of  the traffic signal based upon

the information obtained in the field reviewed. Prepare the final list  for intersection evaluation for City

review and approval.

2. Traffic Data Collection

a. Collect 12-hour  turning movement counts at  the selected study intersections (6:00 AM to 6:00 PM).

The data collection will include vehicles (cars, buses, and HV%), pedestrians and bicycle volumes.

Data collection to be performed during a typical weekday when schools are in session.

3. Signal  Warrant  Analysis

a. Perform a signal warrant analysis at  the selected study intersections to determine if  the existing traffic

volumes meet  the applicable signal warrant criteria for intersection signalization.

b. The signal warrant analysis will be performed in accordance with the requirements outlined in the

Michigan Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices (MMUTCD).

4. Recommendations Summary

a. Provide summary of  recommendations to the City  that includes the following items

• Existing signalized intersections to remove.

• Alternative traffic control measures and signage

• Other considerations for mitigation measures for multi-modal access and safety

b. Complete a technical report consistent with accepted standards, which outlines the methodologies,

analyses, results, and recommendations of  the traffic study. Work will follow accepted traffic

engineering practice and the standards documented by ITE, FHWA, MOOT, and the City of Pontiac.

c. Upon completion of  the proposed scope of  work, a draft copy of  the study memorandum will be

provided for internal review and comment. F&V will provide one revision to the memorandum based

on comments received regarding the draft and finalize the memorandum for submission. An

electronic copy of  the project report will be provided. A hard copy of  the project report will be provided

upon request, subject  to an additional fee.

D. COMMERCIAL  VEHICLE ROUTE REVIEW

Perform a review of  the existing truck routing in the City. Provide recommendations for a comprehensive truck

routing plan to manage local truck activity and  reduce impacts on the local community. Recommendation to

include a comprehensive truck routing  plan for implementation and enforcement.

1. Background Data

a. Obtain information from the City regarding their existing approved and restricted truck routes.

b. Provide a summary of  the existing truck restriction signage throughout  the City.

c. Collect heavy  truck origin-destination (O-D) data utilizing geospatial telematics data sets (i.e. Replica,

Geotab)

d. Provide a summary of  the existing truck routing throughout the City.

2. Truck Route Assessment

a. Review  the City roadway segments that meet  the minimum required criteria for a truck route

destination. This includes the consideration of national functional classification, Act 51 certification,

bridge vertical clearance, bridge weight limit, and any code restrictions.

b. Develop a scoring system and rank each roadway segment in the City from the least  to the most

preferred truck routes based on specific criteria. The criteria include adjacer_it land uses, multi-modal

impacts, environmental (noise, vibration) impact, connectivity, compatibility for  truck usages, and

roadway and bridge conditions.

c. Prepare a list of  potential routes that may be considered for  truck routes.

P51754 City of Pontiac - Traffic Engineering Ser,ices Proposal 4w28-2026

Traffic Engineering Services Proposal City of Pontiac, Michigan

April 28, 2026 j Page 4 of  4

d. Compare the existing routes utilized by  the trucks to the potential truck routes based upon the scoring

criteria.

3. Route Recommendations

a. Review the potential routes and prepare recommendations for designated routes that included the

following route classifications:

• Recommended Route: Designated truck  through routes

• Recommended Restricted Route: Designated local access truck routes restricting pass

through truck  traffic

• Emergency Route: Undefined

• Route Designated by Others: MOOT and RCOC designated truck routes

b. Identify potential improvements to the roadway network to restrict access for trucks using those

This letter  serves to notify  you that CivicServe, Inc. is the sole source provider of  the CivicServe Economic Development

suite of  software  for local governments.  The platform has been built  to support  the entirety  of  the economic

development lifecycle process, including complex  workflows, digitizing local economic development reporting

requirements, and automating compliance  with agreement  obligations.  CivicServe is the only purpose-built platform

service by  checking the live status of  their  applications & projects online. Stimulate new  development  with our

available property promotion  that  showcases  your  community  to  developers.

• Back-end  economic  development  CRM, project  &  incentive  management: Allows City  staff  to manage  the full

lifecycle of incentive programs and projects, track leads, automate  the tracking of  agreement  obligations and

submissions, log and track interactions  with businesses and developers, and log site  visits & BRE visits with survey

results.

• Monthly  business intelligence  updates: Keep your  CRM automatically updated  with fresh business data every

month  and stay informed of  critical changes  to  your businesses  with monthly  alerts.

• Automated  compliance  notifications  and public  portal  documentation  submission: Developers, businesses, and

residents will be  able  to  supply  compliance & KPI documentation directly  to  the City  seamlessly  through the public

portal.

• Property  management  portal: Manage  available properties,  track  submissions of interest  from the public portal,

and collect property  information  from brokers.

• GIS integration:  The City  will be able  to allow users  to submit  online applications  and pre-screen users by  address

to  allow  them to understand  which programs  they  are  eligible  for.

• Real-time  reporting &  analytics: Report real-time information related to program performance, projects, business

outreach activity,  and more. Assuming the City provides CivicServe the necessary  data  from its GIS system,  the City

will be  able  to report on activity,  data,  and projects by  incentive district, council district,  zip code, etc.  within the

platform. Leverage the system's custom reporting wizard to create complex  custom reports to meet  local & state

reporting requirements.

data,  and processes into a single environment, eliminating spreadsheets, and other  error-prone manual processes. No

Overview

The Courts and L Law E Enforcement M Management I Information System (CLEMIS) A Authority is a r regional

public safety i information m management s system serving approximately 250 g governmental e entities across

ten counties i in M Michigan. Originally c created b by Oakland C County in 1968 t to enable criminal j justice a and

public safety agencies to share data electronically, CLEMIS was formally established as a separate

legal entity effective February 1, 2026, pursuant to the Urban Cooperation Act of 1967 and an

Interlocal Agreement b between Oakland County, the Charter T Township of B Bloomfield, and the Ch_arter

Township of W White Lake.

The City o of P Pontiac h has an opportunity t to j join t the CLEMIS Authority as a P Participating P Public A Agency,

which will provide the City's Code Enforcement Department with access to the CLEMIS system for

courts and law enforcement management information services. Participation requires City Council

adoption of a an Authorizing Resolution, execution of a  a Participation Agreement, and execution of a  a

CLEMIS Main Services Agreement (MSA).

Funding

Funding for CLEMIS participation is available through the General Fund. The General Ledger

Number being utilized is 101-733-818.000. The annual cost of $ $23,301.00 will be payable beginning

October 1, 2026, for the period October 2026 through September 2 2027. The subscription t term renews

automatically on an annual basis unless terminated b by either p party with at least 30 days' written notice

prior t to the expiration of t the then-current term.

Recommendation

City Services staff r recommend that the City Council approve this resolution authorizing the City of

Pontiac to participate in the CLEMIS Authority Interlocal Agreement and Main Services Agreement,

and authorizing t the Director of C City Services t to execute all necessary documents on b behalf o of t the City.

Approval w will allow t the City's Code E Enforcement D Department t to access t the CLEMIS system b beginning

October 1, 2026, supporting improved data sharing and operational efficiency in courts and law

enforcement m management information services.

Overview

At the February 17, 2026, City Council meeting, Council approved the award of t the Galloway

Lake Park Court Improvements contract to WCI Contractors for $295,781.00 (base bid including

contingency). At the March 3, 2026, City Council meeting, City Council approved the bid add

alternate #1 totaling $112,672.40.

It has since been identified that the Council of M Michigan Foundations approved on May 7, 2026,

the reallocation of f funds for tree removal to fence installation required for the courts. The trees are

no longer required for removal because they were deemed healthy and do not obstruct the Ewalt

Center d demolition or court improvement p project.

The funding source is as follows:

• Bid Alternate #4 total $50,700.00 (Fence Installation)

o $27,289.00 - 101-818-818.000-SPARKG- Other Professional Services

o $23,411.00- 101-818-816.000-Contracted Construction Services

With the reallocation of $ $27,289.00 in grant funds awarded and $23,411.00 from the general fund,

the total project a amount will increase to the not-to-exceed amount of $ $459,153.40

Funding & Contingency

Funding for the Galloway Lake Park Court Improvements Project is available through the Council

of M Michigan Foundations (CMF) and Michigan Department of N Natural Resources (DNR) SPARK

Grant Program. As part of t this contract modification, the project scope is being revised to remove

the tree and brush-clearing component previously budgeted at $27,289.00 and reallocate those

funds to Bid Alternate #4, which provides fencing improvements for the new pickleball courts. Bid

Alternate #4 has a total cost of $ $50,700.00. Of t this amount, $27,289.00 will be funded through the

reallocated project funds originally designated for tree and brush removal. The remaining

$23,411.00 will be funded through the City's General Fund. With the inclusion of B Bid Alternate #1

and Bid Alternate #4, the revised total contract amount for the project shall not exceed

$459,153.40. Adequate grant and City funding have been identified and are available to support

the revised project scope.

Recommendation

Parks and Recreation staff r recommend that t the City Council approve the revised project scope and

authorize the award to WCI Contractors in an amount not to exceed $459,153.40, inclusive of B Bid

Alternate #1 and Bid A Alternate #4.

Approval of t this resolution will allow the City to enhance the project by installing fencing around

the pickleball courts while utilizing existing project funds previously allocated for tree and brush

removal and providing the remaining balance through the General Fund. Staff believe these

improvements will better support the long-term functionality, safety, and user experience of t the

facility and recommend City Council approval.

Overview

Gun violence continues to impact public safety in Pontiac, particularly in high-density residential

areas. The City of P Pontiac has invested r resources and undertaken myriad strategies and initiatives t to

prevent violent crime. The current and previous City Administration and City Council has

established and expanded the Pontiac SAFE violence intervention initiatives and invested in

expanded capacity and responsiveness with our Oakland County Sheriff D Department c contract.

The City of P Pontiac recognizes gun violence as a major contributor t to negative health outcomes, as

well as a negative impact on the quality of l life for our residents, and City Council has recently

adopted a Resolution identified gun violence as a public health epidemic in our community.

The rates of incidents of v violent crime continue to decrease in the City of Pontiac, but we are

committed to further preventing future incidents and further decreasing those rates. The Newman

Lane, Spring Lake, and North Hill Farms apartment complexes have experienced elevated levels of

gun-related incidents. Traditional 911 reporting does not capture all gunfire activity, resulting in

gaps in response and enforcement.

Raven Gunshot Detection Technology provides real-time, location-based alerts that enable faster

and more accurate law enforcement response. The system is now operated under the Flock Safety

platform (Flock Group Inc.). The previous Administration authorized a six-month pilot program

undertaken in collaboration with the Oakland County Sheriff Department, whereby the Raven

Gunshot Detection Technology would be utilized within the City of Pontiac to improve crime

prevention a and crime incident r responsiveness and assess the efficacy of t the t technology for our c city's

needs. According t to the Raven analytics report (May 8, 2026), the system generated 736 total alerts

during the deployment period, including 269 gunshot-related alerts, 212 fireworks alerts, and 19

sideshow alerts.

Raven t technology h has directly supported 11 significant c criminal i investigations, including 2  2 h homicide

cases both solved in large part with Raven-generated evidence, 4 attempted murder cases, and 3

felons in possession of a  a firearm case.

Recommendation

The Mayor's Office recommends adoption of t this Resolution, which would authorize the continued

deployment, operation, and utilization of the Raven Gunshot Detection system within Newman

Lane, Spring L Lake, and North Hill Farms apartment communities in coordination with the Oakland

County Sheriffs Office. We are committed to further improving the safety of our Pontiac

neighborhoods and further decreasing our violent crime rates, and we believe that this is an

appropriate and effective technology to help accomplish those goals.

CITY OF PONTIAC CITY COUNCIL

RESOLUTION A AUTHORIZING CONTINUED UTILIZATION OF RA V VEN

GUNSHOT DETECTION TECHNOLOGY IN THE CITY OF PONTIAC

WHEREAS, the City of P Pontiac is committed t to ensuring t the safety and well-being of a all residents,

businesses, and visitors; and,

WHEREAS, gun violence continues to pose a significant t threat to public safety and quality of l life

within t the City of P Pontiac, particularly in certain high-density residential areas; and,

WHEREAS, the Newman Lane, Spring Lake, and North Hill Farms apartment communities have

been identified as locations experiencing a disproportionate volume of c criminal activity, including

gun-related incidents; and,

WHEREAS, rapid and accurate detection of g gunfire incidents can significantly improve emergency

response times, assist law enforcement investigations, and enhance community safety outcomes

within the City of P Pontiac; and,

WHEREAS, Raven Gunshot Detection Technology is an advanced, real-time system operated

through the Flock Safety platform (Flock Group Inc.) that detects, locates, and alerts law

enforcement t to suspected gunfire incidents using acoustic sensors and data analysis; and,

WHEREAS, the Raven analytics report dated May 8, 2026, reflects the following activity within

the deployment p period: a t total of736 a alerts were generated including 269 gunshot-related alerts, 212

fireworks alerts, and 19 sideshow alerts; and,

WHEREAS, Raven technology has directly assisted law enforcement in 11 major criminal

incidents, including 2 homicide investigations both solved in large part t through evidence collected

by the Raven Gunshot Detection system, 4 attempted murder cases, and 3 felons in possession of a  a

firearm cases; and,

WHEREAS, the Oakland County Sheriffs Office holds the vendor contract w with Flock Group Inc.

for this technology, and the City of P Pontiac's continued participation requires formal City Council

authorization; and,

WHEREAS, the annual cost for continued deployment is $36,000.00, covering designated areas,

with a 24-month contract t total of $ $72,000.00; and,

WHEREAS, the pilot period for this technology has concluded-and the City of P Pontiac seeks to

continue and maintain the Raven Gunshot Detection system within the identified communities to

support law enforcement response and enhance public safety; now,

THEREFORE, B BE IT RESOLVED, that t the Pontiac City Council h hereby authorizes t the continued

deployment, operation, and utilization of t the Raven Gunshot Detection system within the Newman

Lane, Spring Lake, and North Hill Farms apartment communities, in coordination with t the Oakland

County Sheriffs Office, in an annual amount not t to exceed $36,000.00; and further

RESOLVED, that t the Mayor or his designee is authorized and directed to execute any agreements

or documents necessary to implement the continued deployment of t the Raven Gunshot Detection

system on behalf o of t the City of P Pontiac, subject to approval as to form by the City Attorney; and

further

RESOLVED, that the City Clerk is authorized and directed to transmit copies of t this resolution to

the Oakland County Sheriffs Office and other r relevant stakeholders.
